Two compounding defects, both in scope resolution: capture files are named by **session start date**, so a 22:16 night writes all night into yesterday's folder, while the `LINK`/`CLOCK` sidecars **roll on the wall clock** and create a post-midnight folder holding nothing else — and `_current_night` took `max(active)`, which is lexical, so it **always** picked the empty decoy (`active_nights` returns a set precisely because both folders are live; its own docstring says so). `nightqc`'s cross-midnight pooling, built for exactly this, was gated on `if data:` — so the one condition under which it was needed was the one condition under which it refused to run. Fixed in four layers: the resolver ranks by newest **capture-file** mtime (sidecars never break the tie); pooling runs **from the empty side**; `scope_suspect` marks 'everything missing' as a statement about where we looked rather than nine simultaneous hardware faults (nine independent streams across three vendors do not fail in the same second) and suppresses the false gap alert; and every summary now carries `judged_dir`/`searched_dirs`/`data_files` — the tell was already there (`files: 2`, both sidecars) and nothing surfaced it. **§2 records what was NOT wrong**, because every one of those hypotheses was believed during the incident: the runners never died (**a healthy long-lived session logs nothing** — 1 h 44 m of journal silence was evidence of health), no data was lost, and the H10's PMD stall was *caused* by the recovery restart rather than preceding it. A remediation built on those false premises was reverted, leaving the rule that **no automated remediation may consume a QC aggregate**. Replayed against the real bytes: `missing` 9 → **0**, H10 ECG 0 → **3,114,545 rows**. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-28)* |
| [`REM-STAGING-REDESIGN-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md`](briefs/REM-STAGING-REDESIGN-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md) | **REM is under-called ~4× and the rule's SHAPE is why.** Folding a real capture-box night returned **REM = 0 min across 5.5 h of sleep** (with 99.8 % sleep efficiency and zero WASO). Three defects were found and fixed in `9f1edbc` — the chest-ACC motion index was unreachable (computed inside `accExtras` behind `if (… stages && stages.length)`, while `stageSleep` ran *before* `accExtras`: motion does not depend on staging, staging depends on motion); the REM gate was **absolute** (`lfhf > 2.2` on a subject whose epoch-median LF/HF was 1.62, while the RMSSD and HR gates beside it were already night-relative); and the smoother was an **eraser** (an unconditional despiker against a series where Light held 290 of 330 min deletes every isolated minority label by construction — two epochs satisfied the full REM rule and it removed both). Corpus result over 11 nights, measured against `origin/main` in a worktree: motionIndex **296 → 648** of 1020 epochs, nights with REM = 0 **2 → 0**, median REM% **4.5 → 4.8** against a physiological 15–25 %. **The structural zero is gone; the under-detection is not**, and that is an architecture limit: the REM rule is a CONJUNCTION of two gates that do not co-vary — 26 epochs passed the LF/HF gate, 10 the RMSSD gate, intersection **2** against **~3.4 expected by chance**, so P(A)×P(B) can only under-call and no threshold tuning rescues it. Proposes a weighted REM **score** (z(LF/HF) · −z(RMSSD) · −motion · **+respiratory-rate variability** — the missing positive REM signature, derivable from the RSA and EDR respiration ECGDex already computes but whose *variability* never has been) plus bout structure and, sequenced after, a 1-minute staging grid. **§4, rewritten 2026-07-28 after running the oracle:** the earlier claim that no fixture exercises `stageSleep` was too strong — the `§10/§11 spectral` group already drives `genSynthetic({durSec:100*60})` past `longRec`, so staging **executes** on every CI run; what is missing is any *assertion* on its output (a gate blind rather than green, `DEEP-AUDIT-III §2.3`). And the assertions cannot be written yet, because the oracle is wrong about REM: on 72 synthetic epochs **every planted REM epoch is classified Wake, 9/9** — their `hrZ` runs 1.31–2.22, all above the Wake gate's 1.1, and **Wake is evaluated first**, an ordering defect independent of any threshold — while the generator plants REM with LF/HF 0.084–0.154 (implausibly low; real REM is sympathetically dominant) and a motion index of **96/100** (`ecgdex-dsp.js:446` makes REM the second-most-active stage, contradicting REM atonia). The generator models REM as looking like Wake in every feature the classifier can see, so building against it would train toward a false target. **The conclusion that survives: HRV alone cannot separate REM from Wake — motion is the necessary discriminator, not an enhancement**, and a recording without an accelerometer should abstain rather than guess. §5 sets PSG-free acceptance: planted truth, population plausibility, ~90-min cycle autocorrelation, cross-signal consistency with OxyDex desat events. **EXECUTED 2026-07-28 (§4a/§4b/§4c + the LF band):** the Mayer wave was an AR(1) low-pass stepped once per beat (~0.014 Hz, VLF) while its comment claimed 0.1 Hz, so LF/HF measured ~0.1 for every stage and the REM gate could not fire at all; as a real 0.1 Hz oscillation it becomes physiological AND ordered (N3 0.63 · REM 2.43), and it is redistribution not inflation (SDNN 100.7 → 100.6). REM motion corrected to atonia (96 → 26). With the ordering fixed — spectrum leads, motion vetoes — **planted REM recall goes 0/9 → 9/9** and the corpus median REM% 4.8 → 6.5 with no night regressed. The bout guard was built, measured and **not shipped** (inert on the synthetic; took the real night from 10 min to zero). Four known-answers re-pinned knowingly (PRSA DC 7.35→9.62 · AC −7.16→−10.26 · SampEn 0.562→1.03 · consensus 77→67→80). **Still open: the weighted-score detector** — the conjunction yields 2 of 77 epochs on real data, so 6.5% is still short of the physiological 15–25%. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-07-28)* |
| [`ECGDEX-CARDIOPULMONARY-COUPLING-2026-07-30-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ECGDEX-CARDIOPULMONARY-COUPLING-2026-07-30-BRIEF.md) | **The export cites cardiopulmonary coupling; nothing computes it.** Every ECGDex night publishes `apnea.method = "CVHR/cardiopulmonary-coupling proxy (Hilmisson 2019)"`, but there is **no CPC implementation anywhere** — and the field that sentence describes, `apnea.estimatedAHI`, is **null on 39 of 39 nights**. `cvhrIndex` is real (2.1–9.2); the CPC half is a method claim without an implementation, which `CLAUDE.md` §📚 exists to prevent. Argues for building it rather than deleting the claim, because **CPC is the published method for exactly the problem three rounds of `DEEP-STAGE-DESAT-CONFOUND` failed to solve another way** (rmssd AUC 0.546, lfhf 0.477, vlf/lf 0.599 vs break-even 0.684, targeted CVHR band 0.567). Thomas 2005: coherence between HRV and ECG-derived respiration; **LFC is the validated signature of unstable apnea-disturbed NREM**, HFC of stable NREM. **Smaller than it looks** — `cardiorespCoupling` already computes R-peak amplitude → detrended EDR, HR, and puts **both on a shared uniform 4 Hz grid** (`edrU`/`hrU`), and `_fft` exists; the only missing piece is the cross-spectrum/coherence step. **The validation is what makes it different:** every prior attempt was crippled by the label (a desat is a delayed, threshold-gated *consequence* of apnea), and §9.6 bounded that ~50 % of "clean" epochs would need hidden apnea before VLF cleared break-even — but all 39 trio nights now pair to a **device-scored ResMed `residualAHI` (1.1–8.0, 7 abnormal-band)** from 199 parsed CPAP nights, the independent ground truth §9.6 named as the only remaining lever. Pins window/overlap/detrend/min-length as parameters that must be JUSTIFIED not guessed (the 40-beat detrend may erase VLFC at 0.004 Hz — the same class of error as `lfhf` being blind to VLF). **Explicitly NOT a stager change:** §9.4 governs, `Deep` does not move on this brief, and if CPC fails to validate the honest outcome is to retire `estAHI` and the claim rather than fill it with the half we happen to have. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`ECGDEX-CARDIOPULMONARY-COUPLING-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ECGDEX-CARDIOPULMONARY-COUPLING-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **What retiring the AHI proxy left behind.** §10 removed `estimatedAHI` + `riskCategory` from ECGDex — both were `cvhrIndex` wearing AHI's name, units and clinical cut-points (`value` was literally `Math.round(cvhrIndex)`, the ±30 % range invented, the bands AHI's own 5/15/30), against a measured **r = −0.151, p = 0.36** on 39 device-scored nights. Retirement was scoped to the PRODUCER; this holds the rest. **§1 — consumers still read the field from LEGACY exports** (`integrator-dsp:322`, `oxydex-fusion` ×3, `cpapdex-coimport`, `oxydex-app`); all null-guard, but the real gap is a **non-CPAP fusion**, where `:555`'s device-scored `residualAHI` override never fires, so a pre-2026-07-31 export still surfaces the retired proxy as an AHI. Fix by ceasing to TRUST it, not by deleting the defensive reads — tolerating a legacy field is correct back-compat. **§2 — ECGDex has NO committed fixture that exercises the apnea block**: all three regenerated to identical bytes after the export shape changed because none carries `apnea` at all (too short for `longRec`), so GATE B and the equiv leg are blind to the path this whole line of work validated — the GlucoDex 14 h-gap lesson again (an adversarial COMMITTED twin beats a real one). **§3 — `personalize()` is a browser-only derivation layer no gate can see**, which is exactly why §9.5 called `estimatedAHI` "null on every night" while it was live in the app as a KPI, a hero pill and a `<5`-target table row; asks the same question of `ppgdex-profile.js` / `glucodex-profile.js`. **§4 — `surgeEscalationPct`** is the next unvalidated apnea-adjacent number in the same block, and the corpus + harness now exist to test it. **§5 — nothing was promoted into the vacated slot on purpose:** `cpc.hfcPct` (r = −0.408) is a **correlate, not an estimator**, and filling `estimatedAHI` from it would reproduce the retired field's exact error with better inputs. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`DEEP-STAGE-DESAT-CONFOUND-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DEEP-STAGE-DESAT-CONFOUND-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **Called-Deep carries 3.4× the desaturations of Light — N3 should carry the fewest.** Fell out of running `REM-STAGING-REDESIGN` §5's cross-signal falsifier for the first time; the REM answer came back underpowered, the **Deep** one did not. Over the 24 nights `trio-batch` has already computed (night key = *(start − 12 h)*, majority-nocturnal, concurrent sessions only), every OxyDex `desat_event` mapped onto its covering ECGDex stage epoch: **Deep 5.39/h [4.30, 6.67] vs Light 1.57/h [1.35, 1.81]** — exact Poisson CIs, **non-overlapping** — with Wake 2.42 and REM 0.64 [0.17, 1.64] (underpowered, 4 events, recorded as hypothesis not finding). Not one night carrying it: **Deep exceeds Light on 11 of 14** nights with ≥30 min of each, median ratio **3.61**, sign test p≈0.03. **Mechanism tested link-by-link:** apnea → cyclical variation of heart rate → large beat-to-beat swings → elevated RMSSD → the Deep rule (`rmssd > 1.12×median && hr < median`) fires. Epochs containing a desat carry **median RMSSD 40.5 vs 35.1** and are **2.3× more likely called Deep (20.5 % vs 9.0 %)**. **Bounded honestly:** the excess is ~18 of 187 Deep epochs, so CVHR-misreading accounts for **~10 % of Deep minutes, not most of them**. The between-night dose-response is null (`r(ODI-4, Deep%) = 0.003`) — but ODI-4 spans only **0.4–5.2, median 1.9**, all normal-band, so there is no dose variation to correlate against; untestable here, not refuted. **Why it outranks the REM under-call:** REM is visibly, uniformly too LOW and a reader can discount a `heuristic` tier; this one runs the FLATTERING direction — deep-sleep minutes inflated on exactly the epochs where breathing was disturbed, so a user with worsening apnea sees their deep sleep hold up. **§5's settling test RUN 2026-07-29: Deep SURVIVES** — excluding every desat-overlapping epoch moves Deep only 11.5 % → 10.3 % of sleep with the RMSSD gap unchanged (8.1 → 8.2), so the rule is **not primarily reading CVHR** and the effect is bounded at ~10 % of Deep minutes by two independent routes. Desat-overlapping epochs remain **2.4× enriched for Deep** (25.2 % vs 10.3 %), and within them the Deep-vs-Light RMSSD gap shrinks to 4.1 because **Light epochs carrying desats are themselves RMSSD-elevated** (39.9 vs 34.8) — CVHR lifts RMSSD across stages and only the epochs it pushes past `1.12 × median` flip. Honest limit: this cannot separate misclassification from genuine N3-predominant OSA; PSG would. Remaining tests (exclude desat-overlapping epochs; re-run on CPAPDex nights with real AHI spread; key Deep on CVHR's VLF/LF spectral signature rather than raw RMSSD, since RMSSD is raised by vagal tone AND by CVHR and the two differ in SHAPE not size). | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-07-29)* |
| [`DEEP-AUDIT-III-2026-07-26-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DEEP-AUDIT-III-2026-07-26-BRIEF.md) | **An `AUDIT-PROMPT.md` pass over the whole Dex suite — 9 parallel dimension hunters, every finding killed or confirmed by an independent adversarial verifier** (baseline **3903 assertions / 257 groups green · GATE A 9/9 · GATE B 25/25**). **30 candidates → 28 CONFIRMED, 2 REFUTED, 0 unverified; 19 ship with a verifier correction** that changed the mechanism, severity or — in three cases — **the fix itself** (two proposed fixes were proven insufficient or would have *fabricated coverage*). **The point of this pass is dimension 4:** the charter records that the Integrator's fusion arithmetic *"has now been left unaudited by two consecutive passes"* — it was opened here and produced the worst findings. §3.1 the headline `confirmedApneaIndex` **DOUBLES** (7.5/h mild → 15/h moderate) when a second oximeter observes the same apneas, because the desat pool's dedupe is a **1-second stamp bucket** rather than an authority merge — a clinical boundary crossed by adding a *device*. §3.2 `apneaCoupling.real`, documented as "the rigorous verdict", is `lift > 1` with **no significance test**, and under the null lift's median IS 1 — **54 % false-positive rate** (162 of 300 independent-stream trials), a coin flip by construction. §3.4 the respiration block fuses **ECGDex with itself across two different nights** and prints "2 independent estimates (ECGDex + ECGDex) … agreement within the ±2 br/min chest-ACC band". §3.3 every `confirmed_apnea_event` attributes its desat to **OxyDex even when OxyDex is not on the bus**. §3.6 `Autonomic ⟷ glycemic` publishes a number computed from the **ECG slope alone** under a false "Single overlapping night" note. That is one class — **class 11, a consensus over non-independent inputs** — reached five ways in one file. Outside it: §6.3 `parseDeviceHR` reads the **last** column of a real PSL `_HR.txt`, which is **HRV in ms**, so 52 % of a surfaced validation card's "bpm" values are milliseconds (70 % of rows dropped); §4.2 MotionDex measures a confident breathing rate **across epochs where the strap was not recording** (linear interpolation + power normalisation discards the only cue that says "no signal"); §4.1 `sampleHz` = count ÷ span reads **18.7 Hz on a 52 Hz** real file; §4.3 no plausibility bound admits **1.6e38 mg** samples while "Immobile time" reads 20 % on a 96 %-immobile night; §6.1 ECGDex counts an **abstaining** ACC vote as agreement; §5.1 PulseDex's *surfaced* Total Power still breaks the Task-Force identity (the 07-14 fix landed in the DSP only, never the app that renders it); §1.1 `resolveDMY` computes `contradictory` and **all six callers discard it** (one anomalous row moves a night six months); §1.4 the Clock-Contract lint prints "clean across 70 files" while **44 shipped files are outside its scope** — a gate failing OPEN by omission. **§7 records the 2 refuted claims and the real adjacent defect each was hiding** (`_o2DateAnchorMs` fabricates a date from an out-of-range 14-digit filename: month 13/day 32 → a night dated 2027-02-01); **§8 names every uncovered area** (the browser lane entirely, the Integrator's TCH/noisy-OR/Poisson machinery, the DFA/SampEn/PRSA differential). Spine edits re-bundle all 8 apps + all 8 provenance fragments (serialize); **every item moves `computeHash`, so corpus re-verification is owed before release**. **CLOSED 2026-07-29:** the four unclosed punch-list items (§3.6, §4.1, §4.2, §4.3) turned out to have been **fixed on 2026-07-27 and never re-stamped** — but two were **ungated**: §3.6 had no gate at all, and §4.1's was pointed one function away from the defect (it drives `respiratoryRate`, which never calls `sampleHz`). Reverting either reddened **zero** assertions. Both now gated and verified RED; residue in `DEEP-AUDIT-III-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-29)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md) | **The coverage contract exists. Nothing emits it — and the capture nodes are the sparse ones.** Drafted arguing the Integrator needed a coverage model; `986d17e` (DEEP-AUDIT-III §6.2) **shipped one** mid-draft, so this brief no longer proposes it and explicitly builds on it. What survives is sharper. **§2** `integrator-dsp.js` records the assumption *"null for every node that records continuously, which is all of them today"* — **false for the three capture nodes**: a BLE drop opens a NEW segment, and one real night spans **24–47 segments per stream** (2026-07-26: 24 Verity PPG, 7 O2Ring, 3 H10), while `ecgdex-dsp.js`/`ppgdex-dsp.js` contain **zero** occurrences of `coverage:`. The block built for HRVDex's 29-day spot measurements is not emitted by the nodes whose recordings actually have holes. **§3** `segmentsOverlap` is consulted in exactly ONE place — `_mayOverlap`, a *boolean* — while every published **quantity** still comes from the envelope: `recWindow` returns a single `[t0Ms,endMs]`, `totHrs` sums merged envelope intervals → `apnea.overlapHours` → **`confirmedAHI = nConf / totHrs`** and the Poisson chance expectation. So even a node that DID declare sparse coverage would still be divided by envelope hours. **§4 measured:** on **2026-07-23** `trio-batch` computes the gap-aware three-way concurrency as **2.1 h** against the Integrator's `overlapHours` **6.86 h** — **3.3×** — and 07-23 is the **only** night of eleven marked `reportable` (`confirmedAHI 0.29`; on 2.1 h the same 2 events are 0.95/h). Not conservative either way: an inflated denominator understates the index while the same coverage raises the chance expectation, so the two do not cancel and the net sign is night-dependent. **§5 why no gate saw it:** GATE B is static (a wrong denominator reproduces perfectly), the equiv fixtures are single-recording and gapless, the synthetic fusion tests *encode* the assumption, and §6.2's own tests exercise the SPARSE-spot shape, not the FRAGMENTED-continuous one. **EXECUTED 2026-07-28 (part 2 — the emitters).** ECGDex/PpgDex/OxyDex now declare `recording.coverage`, each derived from evidence it was already computing and discarding (ECGDex's dropouts, PpgDex's `relSec` jumps, OxyDex's row stamps), assembled through one shared `DexExport.coverageFromSegments`. The block is **ABSENT, not null**, on a contiguous recording — no hole, no claim — so the fleet re-bundle moved **zero** export bytes. The shape that actually costs a number turned out to be the **MERGED** one: every capture file is internally clean (`gaps = 0` on all 6 ECG + 107 PPG files of 07-23) and the fragmentation lives BETWEEN files, so `tools/trio-batch.mjs`'s session merge is the path that matters — measured there, **6.71 h recorded inside a 23.02 h envelope**. `apnea.overlapCoverage` publishes `{basis, recordedHours, envelopeHours, recordedFrac, segments, declaredBy}` so the denominator is auditable and attributable. Adversarial committed twin `synthetic_ecgdex_h10_gapped.txt` (3 segments in a 60 s envelope) runs as a full equiv/GATE-C leg in CI with no corpus; suppressing the emitter reds 10 assertions including that leg. §7 states what is NOT claimed: the 07-23 finding is not false — on the honest denominator the index is *higher*. Residue → `INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-28)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-28-BRIEF.md) | **What emitting `recording.coverage` turned up.** §1.1 the holes were never INSIDE a file — every capture-host session parses with `gaps = 0`; a night is fragmented because it has 6 ECG and 107 PPG *files*, so the per-file emitter alone would have been **completely inert on the corpus it was written for** and only `trio-batch`'s session merge makes it bite (a fixture that cannot express the failure proves nothing — and neither can a code path production never takes). §1.2 a sample INDEX is not a clock position: after one dropout `idx/fs` is wrong by the accumulated silence, quietly — right count, right total, drifted POSITIONS, which is what an intersection is made of. Open: §2.1 `mergeEcg` and `parseECGText` disagree by one sample about what `gaps[].idx` means; §2.2 only ECGDex has a fragmented committed twin (PpgDex/OxyDex are gated by hand-built test inputs); §2.3 `apnea.overlapCoverage` is published but rendered nowhere; §2.4 HRVDex still hand-rolls its coverage literal beside the shared helper. §3 declines a committed three-node fusion golden (it would pin the Integrator's entire output tree for a question about the denominator) and back-filling the `historical:true` fusions. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md`](briefs/NODE-EXPORT-DURATION-SEMANTICS-2026-07-27-BRIEF.md) | **`recording` publishes BOTH a data duration and a clock end — one scalar cannot answer two questions** (owner-ratified 2026-07-27, option (c)). The three trio nodes disagreed with nothing saying which was right: ECGDex's `durSec` is **data seconds** (`n/fs` → `nnRes.activeSec`), PpgDex's is `(n−1)/fs` over a **gap-filled grid** so it is effectively **wall span**, and OxyDex's `durationMin` is explicitly **span** — on 2026-07-17 that is **8.92 h vs 14.54 h vs 14.62 h for one night**. Both readings are defensible; having both under one name is not. **Measured cost** (2026-07-16…26 corpus): because ECGDex declared data seconds and nothing else, `t0 + durSec` lands short of the true end by exactly the dropout time, so **ECGDex's own events fell outside its own declared window on 11 of 11 nights, by +8 min to +326 min**. **The ruling:** `durSec` (how much signal) *and* `endEpochMs` (where it ends on the clock), neither replacing the other. Two non-negotiables — `endEpochMs` is **READ, never reconstructed** from `durSec`+gaps (that is a guess; Clock Contract §2.6 says null, never fabricated) — and **absent ⇒ today's behaviour**, since `integrator-dsp normalizeFile` already prefers `endEpochMs` over every duration key, so no derived fallback was added for the gapless synthetic path where `t0+durSec` is already exact. **ECGDex SHIPPED**: the parser reads the last row's Phone timestamp (round-trips `2026-06-17T01:12:19.371` → `1781658739371` exactly), `analyze` carries it as a RECORDING property like `offsetMin`, both goldens regenerated, gated by a dropout stub where data and wall span provably disagree (3.08 s vs 5.07 s). **The remaining 7 nodes now defer to `986d17e`'s `recording.coverage`** (DEEP-AUDIT-III §6.2) rather than a second convention: `spanSec`/`recordedSec` is this ruling in a richer shape, `endEpochMs` answers the envelope for a continuously-recording node and `coverage.segments` for one that is not — and `INTEGRATOR-GAP-AWARE-OVERLAP` §2 shows the three capture nodes are in the second category and emit no coverage today. §5 prices the remainder honestly: ~15 emitters, every fixture's `outputHash` moves, all 8 regen tools, 11 bundles and a re-stamp of every `provenance/<App>.json` at once — a coordinated work-unit to schedule, not a drive-by. | Brief *(IN-PROGRESS 2026-07-27)* |
| [`MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **Four defects a 37-night fold found that no single night could** — three of them SILENT (no throw, no red, no `lowConfidence`, a plausible number in the export) and the fourth a string identical on every night ever folded. Source: the 2026-07-28 re-fold of **37 trio nights** (2026-06-10 → 07-27; `2026-07-13` and all of `07-16..27` had never been folded) + **197 CPAP nights** — ~3 minutes of wall clock at 8 jobs, a cost that had been assumed prohibitive. **§0 the result that gives the rest its authority:** CPAPDex reproduces the device's OWN scoring in `STR.edf` — `residualAHI` bias **+0.055 /h**, median |Δ| 0.06, max 0.19; `centralIndex` +0.053 / 0.05 / 0.19 — so the EDF parse and event indexing are validated, and STR.edf is usable as an oracle. **§1 (P0) CPAPDex has never once measured periodic breathing** (`cpapdex-dsp.js:834`): it matches `'Cheyne-Stokes'`/`'PeriodicBreathing'` and sums `durSec`, but the AirSense 11 writes PB as a **paired `CSR Start`…`CSR End` marker with `durSec` 0** — so both the label test and the accumulation model are wrong and `periodicBreathingPct` has exported a measured-looking `0.00` on all 197 nights, on a subject whose residual disease is central-predominant. Pairing the markers reproduces the device exactly (06-11 1296 s / 4.9 % · 06-25 1869 s / 7.5 % · 06-27 924 s / 3.8 % · 07-08 598 s / 2.8 %, against STR's 21/31/15/9 — **STR's `CSR` channel is in MINUTES, not percent**, which is why the two sources look 4× apart if misread). **§1.2 is the part worth keeping: the committed synthetic twin was authored against the PARSER, not the device** — `uploads/20260613_231433_CSL.edf` encodes one TAL `+3000 [dur 180] "Cheyne-Stokes"`, exactly the shape the code expects, so the golden faithfully asserts `periodicBreathingPct: 20` while proving only that the code implements its own assumption; the two committed REAL nights pin `0`, correctly, because neither has a CSR span. Amends CLAUDE.md's adversarial-twin rule: **the twin must be adversarial against the device's real encoding, not a restatement of the parser's.** **§2 (P1) PpgDex publishes impossible HRV and flags it confident** (`ppgdex-dsp.js:2518`): 6 of 37 nights carry whole-record rMSSD **91–188 ms** against a chest ECG reading 26–42 ms on the same night, pnn50 42–58 % vs 4–18 %, all six `lowConfidence: false`. Not beat doubling (mean HR agrees ≤ 0.5 bpm), not coverage (analyzable 96–100 %, correction 2.5–13 %), not session concatenation (every 5-min epoch is elevated; 06-29 median epoch rMSSD 193) — epoch `lfhf` collapses to 0.1–0.6, the signature of an **alternating short/long interval sequence**. The gate is a pure COVERAGE gate and no threshold on it can reach them; the free SHAPE detector `rmssd > sdnnRobust` (impossible over a night, both values already computed at `:2601-2602`) holds on all six and none of the other 31, with 07-01/07-02 sitting just under the line. Matters because PpgDex feeds the Integrator consensus and is the TCH-named culprit on 22 of 37 nights (median σ ECG 0.91 / Oxy 1.09 / **PPG 2.71 bpm**). **§3 (P2) OxyDex integrates a stuck motion column** — on 07-16/17 the O2Ring `Motion` field is never 0 (0.0 % zero-samples vs ≥ 98 % on every other night; 07-18 partial at 18.7 %), so it exports `motionPct 100`, `sleepEff 0`, `arousalIndex 100` and computes every motion-GATED metric (HRV, SampEn, desat baseline) over an empty set, unflagged; 100 % motion is a sensor fault, and the fix shape is the `_durBad`/`durationInflated` precedent in the same file. **§4 (P3) the severity opener has two unreachable branches** (`oxydex-dsp.js:2144-2147`) — 37 of 37 nights read `Moderate burden` while `avgScore` spans 1.19 → 5.21, because `worstScore` is 8–10 on every night with 28 ranked metrics; a decision, not a patch, with a DISTRIBUTIONAL acceptance test. **§6 fences off what is NOT a defect** (`durationMin: null` = the gap guard working · `mode: null` = correct abstention · 7 TCH exclusions · the sub-1 h nights) and **§6.1 the two open questions that are not fixes**: OxyDex's PB detector and the device's CSR disagree (r = −0.21; mean 5.0 PB episodes on CSR nights vs 12.7 on the rest) — §1 is the precondition for examining it; and device AHI does not predict oximetric burden on this corpus (r(AHI, ODI3) = 0.06, r(AHI, hypoxic burden) = −0.05), one subject, a fusion question, not a validated finding. **§9:** a silent defect needs a second opinion rather than a better gate · a constant is a finding (`0.00` × 197, one label × 37 — nobody noticed until the column was tabulated) | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-29 — all four sections merged: §1 PR #527, §2 #528, §3 #529, §4 #530; §3's own prescription was WRONG and is corrected in place. Followed by `MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS`)* |
| [`MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **The residue of executing MULTINIGHT-CORPUS-FINDINGS** — the questions its fixes UNBLOCKED rather than answered, plus the capture-side causes nobody has chased. **§1.1 (measurable only because §1 landed):** OxyDex's periodic-breathing detector and the device's CSR **disagree, with the wrong sign** — mean OxyDex PB episodes is **5.0** on the four nights the machine scored CSR>0 and **12.7** on the 33 it scored CSR=0 (`r = −0.21`), while OxyDex's impression says "CS pattern likely — review CPAP pressure" on 28 of 37 nights against the device's 4. Possibly two different constructs each behaving correctly; currently unexamined, and the advice rests on it. **§1.2:** device AHI does not predict oximetric burden here (`r(AHI,ODI3)=0.06`, `r(AHI,hypoxic burden)=−0.05`; 06-14 AHI 1.11 with burden 16.8 vs 07-23 AHI 8.00 with burden 0.9) — a fusion question for `PAPERS-ROADMAP`, one subject, not to be surfaced in a node. **§2 capture-side:** what broke the O2Ring motion byte on 07-16..18 and then **healed it by itself** is unexplained — the device's own `.dat` was 94–98 % zero on the very same nights, so one decode was right and one wrong on identical data; an intermittent corruption that self-heals is the worst kind to leave, because the next occurrence is silently guarded rather than noticed. Plus the reconnect storm (**42/73/99** fragments on three nights) handed to `VIGIL-RECONNECT-BACKOFF-AND-LINK-COUNT`. **§3 unclaimed data already on the card:** CPAPDex parses past `Arousal` and drops it; `reraIndex` reports a measured-looking `0` where the device emits no RERA label at all (should be `null`); `SA2.edf`'s wired `SpO2.1s` is the drop-free source the fragmented nights argue for. **§4 process:** a brief's fix sketch is a lead not an instruction (**third time** — §3's prescription shipped green and missed one of the two nights it existed for, because the fault was per-SOURCE and a merged night looks healthy by fraction); **`--ours` on `provenance/*.json` during a rebase is a GATE-B trap** (right for bytes, wrong for the ledger — caught as `1513466c… ≠ recorded a290a0…`); **`npm run check` includes `typecheck` and the suite does not** (4231 assertions + both build gates + provenance green locally, then red on `tsc` in CI); same-app units serialize even in separate worktrees; and a mutation check on a new invariant group is worth five minutes — the one here would have passed against the old code | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **`fitClockOffset` estimates each channel separately and then VOTES; pooling instead is measurably better.** Every failure of the current estimator — the arbitrary tie (#589), the corroborated-but-wrong night, the nights that yield no number — is one architectural choice: it discards the joint evidence, then rebuilds a decision by voting on distinct-node counts, throwing away the `peakOverFloor` and CI it computed. Measured on **31 nights re-folded under current code**: cluster vote **22/25** in the 36–42 min band, strongest channel 24/25, strongest non-stage 25/25, **POOLED joint fit 29/29** — and pooling additionally resolves **4 nights no per-channel fit could touch** (06-14, 06-19, 07-05, 07-25), because eight weak channels together carry what none carries alone. The statistic slides ONE candidate offset across the night and z-scores every channel against **its own** chance floor (scale-free, so a dense channel cannot dominate), needing **no allow-list** — a noise channel contributes noise at every lag and cannot move the peak. **Null-calibrated (93 random anchor sets):** per-night Z is *not* a separator (12/31 real nights sit below the null's max 9.6) — the evidence is **concentration**: 29 nights inside a 3.58 min band (2.0% of the search range) the null hits 6.5% of the time, ~10⁻³⁴. Answers the parent's open §2b prediction, which the per-channel fit could not: post-correction **−22.33 / −21.17 min on two independent nights, agreeing within 1.16 min**. Also **retracts** parent §2d's *"consuming only corroborated fits is 7/7 correct"* — 2026-06-15 is corroborated by 2 nodes and reports 1.53 min. **EXECUTED 2026-07-31** — `fitClockOffsetPooled` shipped (27 gated assertions), `runFusion`/`integrator-app`/`trio-batch` cut over, `fitClockOffset` deprecated-not-deleted. Corpus reproduced the headline exactly (**29/29 vs 22/25**, whole corpus 2.3 s) and added the property that matters more: **every night it calls confident is in band (21/21), and every night it does NOT is also in band (8/8)** — the in-run null is conservative, never wrong-directioned. Confidence is the **gap-shuffle permutation p-value**, chosen because it degrades honestly on periodic anchors (which pin the offset only modulo their period). Execution added two things the brief did not ask for: the **planted-offset control** (which caught a 37 s argmax bias inside the ±45 s plateau — the point estimate is now the support centroid) and **`underpowered`/`pFloor`**, after a `nullIters: 10` run returned a clean zero-significant negative that was purely an artifact of 1/(N+1) ≥ 0.09. ⛔ **§8.2 CORRECTED the same day** — the *"21/21 confident nights in band, never wrong-directioned"* claim was an **n=31 artifact**, the same shape as the `7/7` it retracts: on the **maximum 45-night corpus** (§8.4 — +9 exports-only nights, +5 admitted by `--min-overlap 0`, since three-way overlap is a *fusion* precondition the clock fit does not need) it is **26/27**, because **2026-07-23 is confident at 35.18 min**. What holds on 43 pre-correction nights: pooled returns a number on **43/43** (vote 36), **41/43 in band** (vote 30/36), and 15 of the 16 non-confident nights are in band anyway. §8.5 diagnoses the error — not stale exports (the other 8 legacy nights are normal), not a device clock (two channels of the SAME H10 file disagree by 3.58 min), but **half-period aliasing** (half the night's mean apnea interval is 3.47 min) — and deliberately ships **no guard**, because inventing one from a single night is fitting the estimator to its own corpus. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-CLOCK-FIT-FOLLOWUPS-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **Once the clock is pinned, every channel is on one timeline — and the pairs do not say what the ladder says.** Executing the pooled fit produced a *physiology* instrument as a by-product: with each night's CPAP↔wearable offset measured, any channel pair can be swept at 1 s resolution against its own shuffled null. 44 pairs / 30 nights ⇒ **9 significant on ≥5 nights**, with the same-device and IMU↔IMU controls landing at 1 s as they must. The finding: `autonomic_surge` ↔ `movement_onset` is significant on **29/30 nights** (Z 11.3) but its latency is **bimodal — +12 s on 22 nights, −22 s on 7 — with a depletion at simultaneity (10 of 992 deltas within ±5 s)**. The per-night MAD of 3 s hides this; only the pooled delta histogram shows it. **Three explanations tested and rejected**: epoch quantisation (0 % of stamps on any grid), CVHR cycle aliasing (median `periodSec` 20 s predicts modes where the histogram has a hole), detector mutual exclusion (no motion gate in the emitter). Also records that this **contradicts `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d's latency ladder** (which orders movement 30 s *ahead* of the surge) — the ladder being the weaker evidence, inferred through the now-deprecated estimator — and that the CPAP rows are **circular** for latency because they were aligned by a fit those same channels dominate. Explicitly refuses to build a metric or a paper on it until the modes are conditioned on arousal intensity and both fiducials document which instant they stamp. Also carries the parent's one confident error (**2026-07-23**, §3) with a candidate guard — reuse the deprecated estimator's clustering as a **disagreement detector** rather than a selector — held back pending validation against all 45 nights; and records that `trio-batch`'s hard-coded `have.length < 3` trio gate (§4) puts **2026-06-06/07/09/13** permanently out of corpus for want of a third device, though a 2-device night is fittable in principle. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01 — §1 + §3 + §4 + §5-window + §6.2 resolved: the bimodal latency was the FIDUCIAL. `autonomic_surge` stamps the bradycardia TROUGH, not the rebound it is named for, which sits `periodSec` later (median 20 s). Re-measuring the same 30 nights against trough+periodSec turns a bimodal +10/−20 distribution with a hole at simultaneity — **10 of 992** deltas within ±5 s — into **one mode with 330 of 915, 36.1 %**. `tMs` unchanged (published contract, and the trough IS the right CVHR fiducial); `meta.peakTMs` now publishes the rebound. `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d amended, ladder deliberately still not rewritten. **§3 REJECTED with the measurement that rejects it:** the proposed channel-disagreement guard flags **22 of 22 correct confident nights**, because agreeing-channel argmax range (70–9425 s) dwarfs the peak support (0–65 s) — individual argmaxes are noise, which is why pooling replaced the vote. No guard is calibratable either: 2026-07-23’s raw data is gone and the 36 reproducible nights contain **zero** confident-but-wrong. The mechanism is already caught at source — planted periodic anchors → `ambiguous`, confidence withheld, and a planted half-period alias still returns the TRUE offset — now gated. This corpus was never at risk: apnea-train interval CV **1.04–2.18**, and an aperiodic train cannot form a comb. **§4 DONE:** `trio-batch --allow-partial` (default OFF) — the hard-coded 3-device gate is a FUSION precondition the clock fit does not need, and it was dropping **42** nights, not the 4 estimated, every one with CPAP data. 41 fold, 41 fit, **16** clear their own null. Three coupled changes were needed; without the third the flag admitted nights and `printClockFit` still never ran on them. **CPAP clock drift MEASURED** (the §7 open question): all 31 confident offsets against date decline monotonically ~41→38 min across May→July, and a negative slope is present **independently in both subsets** — but **no ppm figure is quotable**: trio-only says −9.0 ppm, partial-only −29.1 ppm over a different span, and the trio slope is barely 2× its own scatter. Nothing computes with a fixed offset, so nothing shipped is affected. 2026-05-31 fits confidently at 25.08 min, 13 min below its neighbours — unexplained, recorded rather than silently dropped.)* |
| [`WEARABLE-SYNC-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WEARABLE-SYNC-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **Nothing ever compared the wearables to each other, and they do not agree.** Every clock estimator in the suite measures a wearable against the CPAP; none compared the wearables with EACH OTHER, and all of them assumed a shared timeline. Measured from the raw accelerometers: H10 vs Verity sits **1.8–4.9 s apart (median 3.3 s) on 24 of 24 phone-captured nights — not one inside 1 s** — and **0.10–0.39 s on all 6 box-captured nights**. A systematic bias on one capture path, eliminated by the vigil box, invisible for months because no code made the comparison. It was invisible for a second reason too: the only existing wearable-to-wearable contrast was the ~30-event `movement_onset` channel, which resolved just 8 of 31 nights and INFLATED two of them to +13/+15 s — figures this brief supersedes with ~7 h of continuous 50 Hz data. Ships `IntegratorDSP.activityEnvelope` + `alignEnvelopes` (windowed NCC of accelerometer norms, lag regressed against time so offset AND **drift in ppm** come from one fit — Straczkiewicz 2021 doi:10.3390/s21144777, BMAR arXiv:2501.16015, Bent 2022 doi:10.3390/jimaging8050120, Knapp–Carter 1976, Theil–Sen, Louis 2010) plus `tools/wearable-sync.mjs` and a 13-assertion gate incl. **planted 400 ppm drift recovered to 417**. Confidence is a **concentration test** with an exact binomial tail, after two recorded failures: a window COUNT called two unrelated nights confident (multiple comparisons), and a usable-FRACTION rejected all six box nights that agreed to 0.2 s (most of a sleeping night has no movement to correlate). Also fixes an NCC that could return **r > 1**. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **The offsets are applied. One finding survives, one does not, one cannot be checked.** Discharges `WEARABLE-SYNC` §6: every cross-device figure in the repo assumed the wearables shared a timeline, and 27 of 33 measured nights are >1 s apart. Verity mapped onto the H10 timeline (offset SUBTRACTED, applied to event times *before* the fit); unmeasured nights **excluded, not defaulted to zero**; drift deliberately NOT applied (median 0.0 ppm, but junk on low-window nights — 1666 ppm on 4 windows — so a per-night slope through four points would add noise). **SURVIVES:** the `autonomic_surge ↔ movement_onset` bimodality — 29 nights, **zero sign flips**, both modes shift by the offset so the separation is invariant (39 → 38 s); previously checked on 7 nights. **WITHDRAWN:** the ordered latency ladder — only 3 channels reach 5 confident nights, medians span 16 s against IQRs of ±20 s that overlap completely, and `desat_event` (the rung the story rested on) never reaches 5. Built from point estimates with no uncertainty, it does not survive its own error bars — the same failure as the retracted `7/7` and `21/21`. **UNCHECKABLE:** no impulse bridges the O2Ring to a Polar, so its offset is structurally unmeasurable and every OxyDex timing figure carries that unknown. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-AND-CLOSURE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DEVICE-DRIFT-AND-CLOSURE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Two devices are never on one clock, and the suite has been assuming they are.** Body-worn devices drift relative to each other by tens to hundreds of ppm — enough to walk past a whole heartbeat inside one night — so every cross-node measurement fitting a SINGLE offset per night reports that movement as noise, poor coupling, or a physiological limit. Reached three times independently, twice by sessions that retracted themselves getting there. **Beat correspondence 5–26 % → 43–98.8 %** (100 % in the best blocks of four nights) once the offset is refit per 5 min; chance control at identical DOF is 22–27 %. **The per-block offset is a PHASE and must be UNWRAPPED** — the search spans several comb teeth, so the argmax falls back one RR whenever the offset drifts past a boundary (15 such jumps on 07-27), and a slope through that measures the sawtooth. **Three-corner closure** (`d(A↔B) ≡ d(A↔C) − d(B↔C)` — free and falsifiable) is what caught the missing unwrap; it holds to **≤7 ppm** where correspondence ≥91 % and blocks ≥86, and fails where the phase is undersampled (754 ppm is a broken unwrap, not a crystal). All intervals are **waveform-derived** (Pan-Tompkins RR · 3-LED optical spine PPI), never device files — required, since the Verity’s `_PPI.txt` is header-only on **107 of 107** files and its `_HR.txt` all-zero. **Control:** through the built-in `ECGDSP.validateRR`, the waveform RR reproduces the H10’s own `_RR.txt` to **0.23 % mean / 1.2 % RMSSD** on the night closure holds best — so the drift is in the CLOCKS, not the beat detection. The PPI side has no hardware control available at all and is currently unvalidatable against a device. **Project-wide:** `fitClockOffsetPooled` fits one offset per night — safe for CPAP (0.24–0.78 s ≪ its 15 s support), **not** for wearable↔wearable at 100+ ppm, and nothing marks the difference; **PAT becomes reachable** (per-block IQR 43–112 ms vs the flat, chance-level 264–330 ms under one offset — `pat-gate` wants ≤60 ms); the **capture host** is the right place to measure offset continuously; and `integrator-tch.js`’s reference-free decomposition, used today for amplitude σ, applies to TIMING once closure holds — per-device jitter rather than pairwise differences. **Method lesson:** three honest chance controls all PASSED while the model was too simple — a control tells you whether you beat chance, never whether you asked the right question. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-01)* |
| ⛔ [`IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **⛔ RETRACTED 2026-08-01 — the title claim is WRONG; RR↔PPI CAN align these devices.** Every measurement here fitted a **single constant offset to a pair drifting up to 123 ppm** — more than one RR interval per night — so a scan correct at bedtime compares the wrong beat by morning. Refitting per 5-min block takes correspondence from the **5–26 %** reported to **43–98.8 %** (best blocks 100 % on four nights), against a same-DOF +1 h control of **22–27 %**. **Beat times identify a clock offset only modulo one heartbeat under a CONSTANT offset** — that comb is real mathematics and still forbids searching harder; what resolves it is a drift term. Started as a correction of a claim made hours earlier (that IBI beats ACC↔ACC as an alignment signal: r = 0.532 vs a 0.032 null, a 16× margin, against ACC 0.29/0.065) — the correlation was real, the conclusion was not, and the 1.3 s lag disagreement between the two methods was the falsification that got reported as an open question. **Then the control used to make that point turned out to have the same defect one level down.** The wrist↔finger table was built from nearest-beat matching, which is bounded by ±RR/2 and therefore CIRCULAR — a linear median of it is undefined near the wrap. Re-measured 2026-08-01 with a lag sweep (`tools/beat-comb-analysis.mjs`, `--selftest` planted-truth + `--dir` corpus), the coincidence curve is a **comb whose period is the mean RR, with teeth of equal height** (94–100 % of peak) — so the offset is ambiguous mod one beat by construction, for any statistic. Consequences: the old "5–26 % of beats correspond" column was just **the comb sampled at zero lag** (reproduced within 2 points on all 6 nights); **2026-07-27 was never defective** — its 326 ms "outlier" was the +420 ms tooth, and it has the HIGHEST correspondence of the six (2.54× chance); and near-zero medians do **not** prove the timebase sound — those are simply the nights too flat to form teeth. **Why ACC↔ACC works and this cannot:** an activity envelope is APERIODIC, so its cross-correlation has one peak; a beat train is periodic, so its coincidence curve cannot. **Align on aperiodic features.** **Postscript 2026-08-01 — the two-stage escape route is CLOSED too:** intervals could in principle refine *within* a beat once a coarse method picks which beat, but that needs the coarse stage inside **±0.6 s** and the pooled clock fit resolves to **15 s** — a **25× gap**. Both ways to sharpen it were tested on the same 36 nights and **neither helps**: `autonomic_surge` at its rebound (22→21 confident, support unchanged) and `desat_event` at `onsetTMs` (support 15→**20 s, worse** — a conceptually sharper instant that is empirically noisier). Pooling absorbs a single channel’s offset, which is what pooling is for. Survives: the comb itself, the `autonomic_surge` fiducial finding (event channels over ±60 s — drift of 2–3 s cannot make a 34 s bimodality), and the CPAP work (different pairing, intra-night drift ≪ its 15 s support). **The lesson that cost most:** a chance control tells you whether you beat chance, not whether your model is too simple — the original circular-shift null was honest and passed while one number was being fitted to a system needing two. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01, §Retraction)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Every gate is green and five of eight nodes cannot hand the Integrator its currency.** The suite was driven end-to-end in real Chrome the way a user drives it — files through the actual `<input type=file>`, exports by clicking the actual buttons — on the most complete night in the corpus (2026-07-26, 208 MB H10 ECG + `_RR` + `_ACC`, O2Ring `.dat` + BLE CSV + 46 MB finger PPG, ten ResMed EDFs). **Every node computed correctly** (ECGDex: 208 MB in 10.6 s at 433.4/433.7 min beat coverage). **Five then could not give the user the file:** GlucoDex `⬇ JSON` threw `GLUDSP.glucoCells is not a function` — the function was single-sourced into the DSP by `71b89df` to stop the app’s copy drifting and never PUBLISHED on `global.GLUDSP`, so the app threw while `glucoBuildNodeExport` (lexical scope) kept the gates green for five days; MotionDex enabled `#mxExport` inside a `display:none` bar it never `.show`s (rect 0×0, click rejected "Element is not visible") — the one node whose bar is static markup, so it never inherited the `.show` every other node writes with its buttons; HRVDex made `.mobile-nav` permanent at all widths (z 500) but only ever lifted `#exportBar` (z 400) inside `@media (max-width:1080px)`, so **every export button is pointer-intercepted at ≥1200 px**; and **PulseDex + HRVDex both define `exportGanglior()` wired to no control at all** — so the Integrator’s own remediation text ("re-export via the node’s *Ganglior* button") named a button that did not exist. **OverDex additionally loses OxyDex silently:** `classify()` asked the adapter registry before the node-export sniff, and `adapters/oxydex-spo2.js` claims `/oxydex/i` on the FILENAME at 0.95 — so `OxyDex_*.json` was fed to the CSV row parser, died as "no usable SpO₂ rows parsed", and was never retried (`adapters/libre-cgm.js` carries the same `/glucodex/i` rule). Same six files: Integrator fused 6 nodes / 1 finding, OverDex 5 / 0. **The shape of the bug is the finding** — every gate verifies `compute()`; **none presses a button**, so the app-layer export path rotted in five places independently. Also records, deliberately **unfixed**, a **~42 min CPAP clock offset** (OxyDex desats vs CPAPDex apneas peak at −2510 s: **23/33 paired vs 3/33 at zero**, chance ≈8; the O2Ring’s BLE and `.dat` sources agree with each other to −15 s, 13/13) that the Integrator **found and vetoed** — `peakOverFloor 3.89` against a threshold of **4**, and an agreement test that demands corroboration from ECGDex, which emits sleep stages and autonomic surges and cannot witness a respiratory event.  **EXECUTED** — all five fixed and re-verified in Chrome on the same night: the Integrator now fuses **8 nodes / 449 min** (was 6, with PulseDex at 0 events and HRVDex unstamped), OverDex **8 recordings / 1 finding** (was 5 / 0). Ships the gate that was missing: an `exportBarProbe` in render-coverage that asks whether `#exportBar` is displayed and whether `document.elementFromPoint` at each button’s centre lands on that button — a hit-test, because a z-index regression is invisible to `offsetParent`/`getBoundingClientRect`, which is exactly how §3 walked past every structural check. **The gate found three more defects within minutes:** MotionDex had **no render-coverage leg at all** (its bundle was never booted — how a `display:none` bar survived from birth); its **▶ Demo button had never worked** (`MOTIONDSP.genSynthetic is not a function` — `window.MotionDex` published that name, `MOTIONDSP` did not: §1 again in another node); and **GlucoDex’s render-coverage leg was HOLLOW** — on a bare page with no data it scored 16 numeric tokens against `minNums:15`, matched all four of CV/GMI/range/glucose **in the empty state’s own help text**, and cleared `minChars:1500` at 2832 chars, so every assertion in it passed on an empty app. Legs now settle on `#exportBar.show`, the one condition an empty app cannot fake. **Negative-controlled:** removing the §3 CSS fix reds exactly one leg (HRVDex 27/28) — a gate never seen to fail is not evidence. Gates: **4723/4723 zero skips** with `DEX_UPLOADS` on the real corpus (the GATE-C legs actually ran; no export bytes moved), 3 fixtures re-verified via `verify-fixtures.mjs`, browser suite **4719 / 327 groups** (+42 assertions, +1 leg). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **The Integrator already contained the better clock instrument, wired downstream of a veto.** `trio-batch` corrects the CPAP clock on every night it can measure; the Integrator, on the same evidence, corrected **1 night in 24** — because `fitClockOffsetPooled` only ran for a node the COARSE detector had already accepted, and then only to REPORT, while the applied shift came off the coarse 30 s grid. Measured on the 24 trio nights carrying CPAP EDFs: coarse finding **1/24**, pooled confident **19/24**, pooled offset in the documented 30–50 min band **24/24** (37.6–41.7 min). **The veto is not the threshold** — `minPeakOverFloor` is 4 and these nights score **5–12**; it is the ALL-PARTNERS-MUST-AGREE clause, which lets **ECGDex** (sleep stages + autonomic surges, blind to a respiratory event) discard the night. *(This corrects the parent `EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE` §7, which blamed a 0.11 shortfall seen on one night.)* The fit now runs for every dated node and supplies the applied, **sub-second** offset (#624). **After: 19/24 corrected, all CPAPDex, all in band, zero mis-attributions**, the 5 abstentions being nights the fit is not confident. **Attribution honesty:** ranking by |offset| blamed OxyDex/ECGDex for the CPAP’s 39 min (right magnitude, wrong side), and ranking by corroboration breadth failed too because `nNodes` is **3 for every node on almost every night** — the fits are symmetric, so **the statistic cannot decide the side**. It is decided instead by the physical asymmetry the repo already documents (wearables share one disciplined host clock at 0.10–0.39 s; the ResMed has none), so only a node named **un-disciplined** is corrected. **States what it does NOT fix:** on 2026-07-26 desat pairing goes **3/33 → 12/33** but the **199 s residual** exceeds the −15…+60 s coupling window, so DESAT MATCH RATE stays 0 % — the pooled fit averages channels that carry real physiological latency, and the next step is a NARROWER fit on the channel pair a coupling actually scores. **PAT answered with a number:** plateau **median 20 s / max 85 s** against `pat-gate.js`’s **60 ms** bar — ~330× too coarse, so event-coincidence alignment can never feed PAT; that needs `pat-align.js` ACC anchors, and even those sit at 0.10–0.39 s. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`CLOCK-CLOSURE-THREE-SOURCE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CLOCK-CLOSURE-THREE-SOURCE-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Three clocks must close to zero. On six real nights they never do — and once with every leg confident.** A pairwise clock fit cannot check itself; with three interval sources `d(A,B)+d(B,C)+d(C,A) ≡ 0`, so a non-zero **closure error** proves one measurement is wrong **with no reference clock**. Measured on H10 chest RR + Verity wrist PPI + O2Ring finger PPI over **six nights**: closure spans **−6 to +101 ppm**, never zero. **H10↔Verity is reliable every night** (77–100 % correspondence vs 13–22 % chance); **the O2Ring is night-dependent, not simply worse** — 2–54 % on five nights but **95 %/83 % on 2026-07-28**, correcting the single-night impression; and **drift is not a hardware constant** (−7.3 → 96.6 ppm across six nights, so no stored calibration would hold). **The finding that justifies the test:** on 07-28, where all three legs clear their own chance control, closure still misses by **58.4 ppm** — per-leg confidence is necessary and **not sufficient**, and only closure sees it. **The blind spot, found by a control that REFUSED to fire:** a clock stepped 900 ms mid-night left closure at −0.10 ppm, because a wrong clock is measured *faithfully* by both its pairs and cancels — **closure tests the MEASUREMENTS, never the clocks**, and is structurally incapable of catching a bad clock both pairs agree about. Only degrading a source until its FITS broke made it fire (−50.6 ppm, both weak legs named). Tolerance scales to the legs’ own drift magnitudes; <3 sources refuses rather than guesses; printed per night by `trio-batch`. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`WEARABLE-DRIFT-FIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/WEARABLE-DRIFT-FIT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **One number cannot hold a night: the wearables drift 87 ppm, and fitting for it takes correspondence from 16 % to 89 %.** Executes `ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT` §3.7, whose 90 % finding had been **recorded and not used**. `fitClockDrift` refits the offset in 5-min blocks and regresses it against time so **offset AND drift come from one fit** — the shape `alignEnvelopes` already uses for ACC envelopes, applied to beat times. **No contract change and no raw files:** `timeseries.rr.tSec` / `timeseries.ppi.tSec` were on the bus all along, and only beats each node says it OBSERVED are used (99.7 % of RR, 97.5 % of PPI). Real night via `trio-batch`: **80 ppm (2.13 s over 444 min), offset −0.12 s, corr 89 % vs chance 21 %, IQR 52 ms** — where a constant-offset fit reports 16 %. **Gated by PLANTED recovery** (−500 ms/0 ppm and +300 ms/−60 ppm both recovered **exactly**; a planted 250 ppm flattens to 33 and is **declared** out of range via the published `maxDriftPpm`). **The controls found two real defects, fixed not shipped:** argmax on a flat plateau (a **~330 ms** offset bias — replaced by a support centroid, the same fix `POOLED-CLOCK-FIT` made after its own 37 s argmax bias), and a block offset regressed against the block START rather than its midpoint, tilting drift by half a block. Ships `chanceCorrespondence` beside every result because the fit maximises the statistic it reports — `confident` demands ≥2× over its own control. `medianIqrMs` 52 ms sits inside `pat-gate.js`’s ≤60 ms bar (**not** a PAT pass — coupling ≥55 % and a real pulse-arrival median remain separate). Called from `trio-batch`, deliberately **ungated from `--cpap`** since it needs no CPAP anchors; **not** wired into `runFusion`, whose recs deliberately drop `timeseries` to avoid multi-MB retention — that trade-off gets its own work-unit. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/ENVELOPE-ANCHOR-EXPORT-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Anchors are too sparse a carrier: 75 movements a night, 3 of them shared.** Builds the pipe `INTEGRATOR-POOLED-CLOCK-APPLY` §6 identified — the Integrator ships `alignEnvelopes`/`PATAlign` (good for **0.10–0.39 s**) and can never use them, because **no node-export carries anything mechanical**. Built end-to-end: `DexExport.motionAnchors` reusing `PATAlign` (no second copy), emitted by ECGDex (H10 chest ACC) + PpgDex (Verity limb ACC) from inside `analyze` where the raw rows are in scope, `pat-align.js` co-loaded and classified, goldens regenerated, suite green at **4847**. **Then measured, and backed out.** On 2026-07-26: ECGDex **75** anchors, PpgDex **67**; anchor-list match gives 13/75 at ±500 ms (chance ≈0.2 — real) but only **3** windows clear `minCorr`, median −1512 ms, **MAD 270 ms**. The estimator is sound — a planted 100 ms shift is recovered as **99.94 ms** — the SAMPLING is not: a chest strap and an arm band share few discrete movements. `WEARABLE-SYNC` reached 0.10–0.39 s with **windowed NCC over the whole 50 Hz envelope**; anchors keep ~10² bins of ~10⁵, so the compaction that made the field affordable is what made it uninformative. **Backed out rather than shipped** because `ganglior.node-export` is a published contract that should change once, and a field with no working consumer is the defect #636 had just fixed. Leaves the real design question with sizes: 50 ms bins = 576,000 values (impossible), 4 Hz = ~700 KB (too big), 1 Hz = ~170 KB (affordable, not sub-second), anchors = ~30 KB (too few pairs) — and argues the **capture host** should measure the offset directly, since it holds both raw streams and already disciplines both Polar clocks.  **§3.5 CORRECTS its own opening:** the claim *"no node-export carries anything mechanical"* is wrong in a way that matters — the exports already carry **explicit per-beat times** (`timeseries.rr.tSec` / `timeseries.ppi.tSec`; 22,460 and 22,145 beats on 2026-07-26), a sub-second shared channel already on the bus needing **no contract change**. Measured: interval-sequence correlation **r = 0.411**, but the implied time offset has **MAD 22 s / IQR 47 s** because dropped beats accumulate an index error, and only **15.9 %** of beats have a counterpart within 100 ms — an independent reproduction of `IBI-ALIGNMENT-LIMIT`’s **5–26 %** on a night it did not use. PAT-window deltas median **336 ms** (physiologically right, clears `pat-gate.js`’s `physical` check) with **IQR 265 ms** — failing the ≤60 ms bar by **4.4×**, exactly as 16 % correspondence predicts. **The limit is physiological, not plumbing**, so one direction closes: do not build an RR↔PPI aligner.  **§3.6 tests a TWO-PASS scheme** (coarse ACC lock dissolves the mod-RR ambiguity, beat trains then refine) — right shape, still fails, and **the control is the finding**: best IQR over a ±3 s sweep is **264 ms at −0.90 s**, while the same statistic **shifted a full hour away** gives **330 ms** at the same ~46 % coverage. Being an hour wrong looks like being right; the IQR is flat everywhere. At ~16 % beat correspondence a 640 ms window with ~1 s beats fills by chance at every offset, so the measurement is the WINDOW’S width, not a pulse transit. Without the control the sweep reads as an alignment. What would move pass 2 is **better beat correspondence, not a better search** — restrict to high-SQI PPG epochs; `PAT-FEASIBILITY`’s `coupling >= 55 %` already encodes that, and this night is nowhere near it.  ⛔ **§3.7 RETRACTS §3.5 AND §3.6 — the 16 % was DRIFT, and RR↔PPI works.** Both measured a **drifting pair with a single constant offset**. Refitting locally in 5-min blocks on the beats each node says it OBSERVED (99.7 % of RR, 97.5 % of PPI): **median local correspondence 90.6 %** (best 99.6 %) against a **+1 h chance control at 21.3 %** carrying the same search degrees of freedom. Beat correspondence is **~90 %, not 16 %**. The pair drifts at **5.2 ms/min = 87 ppm → 2,264 ms over 435 min** — two seconds, larger than an RR interval, so a constant-offset match walks off the correct beat partway through the night, which is exactly the signature §3.5 saw (strong interval-sequence r, hopeless implied offset at MAD 22 s) and §3.6’s flat sweep. **Per-block IQR 43–112 ms, median ≈50 ms — inside `pat-gate.js`’s ≤60 ms bar**, so the precision PAT needs is REACHABLE on this pair (not a PAT pass: coupling ≥55 % and a real pulse-arrival median remain separate). The correct instrument already exists — `WEARABLE-SYNC`’s `alignEnvelopes` regresses lag against time so **offset and drift come from one fit**. **Method note:** both wrong conclusions came from one omission — no drift term. The chance control caught §3.6’s non-result but cannot catch a MODEL that is too simple; a control tells you whether you beat chance, not whether you asked the right question. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-01 — §3.5/§3.6 retracted)* |
| [`POOLED-FIT-CHANNEL-DISPERSION-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/POOLED-FIT-CHANNEL-DISPERSION-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **Three channels agree on the CPAP offset to under a minute, and the pool is not weighting them.** Measures WHY the applied pooled correction still sat **199 s** from what desat↔apnea needs. Per-channel `ownOffsetSec` vs the CPAP across 24 nights: `motion_artifact_segment` **37.67 min (MAD 0.67)**, `autonomic_surge` **38.08 (MAD 0.25)**, `desat_event` **39.17 (MAD 0.50)** — versus `periodic_breathing` 36.08 (MAD 3.75), `stage_light` 24.92 (**MAD 35.75**), `hrv_drop` 27.67 (**MAD 62.08**). **The top three are a physiological ladder, not scatter** — movement → surge → desaturation, spanning **90 s** with MADs of 15–40 s, exactly the order circulation time predicts. So the 90 s spread is SIGNAL: the clock offset is common and each channel adds its own latency, which means a single pooled median hands a desat-scored coupling an offset biased ~1–1.5 min early — most of the residual. Also finds that `fitClockOffsetPooled` has **NO per-channel stability gate at all** (its deprecated sibling gates on bootstrap-CI width, with a note that stage impulses "clear a peak-over-floor test at essentially arbitrary lags"), so `stage_light` enters untested and joins the winning cluster **9 of 19** nights. **Deliberately changes nothing:** the obvious cut is refuted by its own measurement — a uniformly-scattering channel would land in the 180 s agreement window ~**7 %** of the time and `stage_light` lands there **47 %**, so it carries real signal on most nights and pooling exists because channels are "individually weak and jointly decisive". Lists three directions (per-coupling offset — **with an explicit circularity warning**, latency-aware pooling, importing the CI gate), each needing a null calibration. Explicitly does **not** revive `WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED` §2’s withdrawn wearable↔wearable ladder — different anchor, different pairing, better resourced.  ⛔ **§1 RETRACTED the same day.** The ladder is **not new** — it is `CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW` §2d re-derived (37.5/38.0/39.5 there vs 37.67/38.08/39.17 here, **same CPAP anchor, same channels**), and `WEARABLE-SYNC-APPLIED` §2 had already **withdrawn** it for insufficient power. §1’s defence — *"a different comparison … against the CPAP anchor"* — was **false**, since §2d is CPAP-anchored too; it was constructed rather than checked. The withdrawal’s reasoning also applies unchanged: it retracted on *"IQRs of ±20 s that overlap completely"*, and §1’s own MADs are **15–40 s** — the same dispersion, so a 90 s span across three rungs establishes no ordering, and calling it *"signal, not error"* repeats the exact point-estimate-without-error-bar failure both prior briefs retracted. The measurement **also duplicated existing tooling** (`trio-batch.mjs printClockFit` already prints per-channel z per night). **What survives:** §2 — `fitClockOffsetPooled` admits channels on event count **and nothing else**, while its deprecated sibling gates on bootstrap-CI width, so `stage_light` enters untested with ±35.75 min scatter and joins the winning cluster 9/19 — and §3’s measured refusal to cut it (**47 % vs a ~7 % chance rate**). The 199 s attribution drops from measured cause to untested hypothesis. | Brief *(PROPOSED 2026-08-01 — §1 retracted)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **The hollow leg was not one leg — two of six were vacuous and a third missed by a single token.** Answers the parent’s open question by measurement: each render-coverage leg’s OWN predicate replayed against its bare bundle with no data injected. `renderCoverageApp` settles on `_numTokens >= minNums && labels.some(…)`, and on the EMPTY app **GlucoDex scores 16/15 with all four of CV/GMI/range/glucose matched in its own "Load a CGM export…" help text**, **PpgDex 17/12** — both fully vacuous — while **HRVDex misses by ONE token (11/12)**, OxyDex by two, PulseDex by three. `minChars` separates nothing: five of six empty apps already exceed 1500 chars. The root cause is the metric, not the numbers — a label regex over `innerText` cannot tell a rendered result from an instruction telling you how to produce one. The parent’s `#exportBar.show` settle is the right condition and not by luck (**false on all six bare bundles**), so all six are guarded today; the hole left was the NEXT leg, so an unguarded full leg now **reds** instead of passing quietly. **The three bespoke legs were audited too and need no change** — ECGDex settles on ≥6 populated VALUE CELLS + `bpm`, CPAPDex on a revealed `#resultsView` + ≥2 painted canvases, IntegratorPB on a named finding. An earlier pass of this audit **wrongly flagged ECGDex as hollow** by applying the generic body-token proxy instead of its real predicate; the correction is the keeper — **counting value cells is materially stronger than counting numbers in `innerText`**, and is the principled end state for the generic legs if the export-bar guard ever proves insufficient.  **Also WITHDRAWS a parent finding.** Parent §11 logged "`OxyDex .dat` emits no `recording.coverage` while its CSV path does" as a defect; checked before acting, it is **correct behaviour** — `oxyCoverage` returns `null` on a contiguous night by design ("contiguous ⇒ no claim to make (DexExport contract)"), and the two paths differ because the RECORDINGS differ, not the code: the `_STORED.dat` is **442.9 min of uninterrupted device memory** (nothing to declare) while the BLE `_SPO2.csv` is **149.4 min with a real 2-segment dropout**. The Integrator’s "envelope basis · 100 %" is right for a contiguous night. **The generalisable error:** a defect was inferred from a difference between two OUTPUTS without checking whether the two INPUTS differed — two ingest paths disagreeing is only a bug if they were given the same recording. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-II-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **OverDex advertised an adapter it could not feed and a node it never booted.** `resmed-edf` sits in OverDex’s own nine-adapter UI list, and a dropped ResMed night routed correctly then died — *"unusable frame: EDF is binary + multi-file — pass `ctx.buffers`… the text argument is not used"*. **Two stacked gaps, neither in the adapter** (correct since it was written): (1) `ingest()` called `readText` on everything, so the documented `ctx.buffers` hatch — the same mechanism `oxydex-spo2` uses for `ctx.parseCSV` — was never populated by anyone; bytes are now read ALONGSIDE text for `.edf` only, and the set is decoded **ONCE** (one night = one EDF set of BRP/PLD/SA2 + EVE/CSL grouped by the adapter’s §F4 rule) with every other file marked **folded**, never blanked — a consumed file must say so. (2) With bytes flowing it died again on a realm **one script short**: the `__DEX_NAMESPACED__` block listed six DSPs and not cpapdex, while `signal-orchestrate.js` already had `cpapHost()` / `emitCpapNodeExport()` / `cpap` in `_HOSTS` gated on `canEmit('cpap')` — the orchestration was complete, the realm was not. Real night: **CPAPDex · 29 events, fused**; previously nothing. **Records, deliberately unresolved, that the two ingest paths disagree by one session** — same ten files, app window starts **21:08:19 / 28 events**, OverDex **21:02:25 / 29 events**, identical end: OverDex keeps a ~6-minute leading mask-on session the app drops, which moves therapy hours and the AHI denominator, not just a count. Unobservable until now (the path did not run), and picking a side from one night is the error `POOLED-CLOCK-FIT` §8.5 refused. Also records the identical missing co-load in `Data Unifier.src.html` as **owed**, since fixing one sibling orchestrator and not the other is how they drift. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-III-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-III-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **CPAPDex was silently discarding 76 EDF files across 16 of 199 nights.** `FOLLOWUPS-II` recorded, unresolved, that the two CPAP ingest routes disagreed on one night (app 28 events from 21:08:19, OverDex 29 from 21:02:25) and refused to pick a side from a single night. Resolved on the corpus — and the answer is worse than a disagreement: **there were two implementations of one rule, and the app’s loses data.** `cpapdex-app.js` clustered on a **>15 MINUTE** gap then did `c.files[e.type] = e` — *"last-wins on duplicate type"* — so when a second therapy session starts under 15 min after the first (mask off, bathroom, mask on) ResMed writes a second complete BRP/PLD/SA2/EVE/CSL set and **the app drops the first one entirely**: its scored apneas vanish with no error and a lower AHI. Replayed over the real SD card (**199 night folders, 1008 EDF files**): **76 files silently discarded across 16 nights — 8.0 %**, worst night 2026-04-23 seeing **2 sessions where the rule sees 6** (14 files dropped). The §F4 header had already warned of exactly this failure at ±60 s ("8 sessions in the ~180-night reference corpus"); the app’s window is **fifteen times wider**. **Resolved by deleting the duplicate, not by choosing** — the rule now lives once on `CpapEdf.groupSessionSets`, the shared reader both surfaces already load, with the adapter delegating and keeping its public name. On 2026-07-26 the app goes **1 session / 28 events / 7.27 h → 2 / 29 / 7.33 h**, matching OverDex exactly because they now run the same function. **Fixture immobility PROVEN, not assumed:** synthetic goldens regenerate byte-identical, and the two real-recording fixtures the regen tool had to SKIP (06-12, 06-16 — and a skip is exactly where a stale fixture hides) were grouped under both rules straight from the card: identical set count AND identical file membership. Also closes `FOLLOWUPS-II` §4 (the Data Unifier’s missing cpapdex co-load — patching one sibling orchestrator and not the other is how they drifted). Gate extended with the delegation + real-corpus shape, and **negative-controlled** (removing the `!byType` clause reds it). | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-IV-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md`](briefs/EXPORT-PATH-UNREACHABLE-FOLLOWUPS-IV-2026-08-01-BRIEF.md) | **PpgDex exported an O2Ring finger night as a wrist-worn Polar Verity.** The last unfixed defect from the end-to-end Chrome run that opened this family: `ppgdex-app.js` **hardcoded** `device: 'Polar Sense'`, so a real export contradicted its own source field — `source: "Wellue_O2Ring-S_…_PPG.txt"`, `device: "Polar Sense"`. Every PpgDex export ever taken from the app named a Polar Verity whatever the instrument was, while the DSP had already worked out the truth (`site: 'finger'` for a single-channel pleth, because *"a 1-column pleth IS an O2Ring ring"*). **A provenance field naming the WRONG instrument is worse than an absent one** — an absent field prompts a question, a confident wrong one ends the enquiry — and it mislabels the body site, which the morphology tiers depend on (wrist literature does not license a finger reading). **Same two-builders shape as the rest of the family:** the DSP builder gained `site`/`siteSource` in #626 and the app’s never did, because **nothing executes the app’s builder** — the equiv legs run `PpgDex.compute`. The app block now matches its sibling: device follows `r.site`, plus `site` + `siteSource` (the honesty carrier — `device-default` = inferred from the optical layout, `declared` = the wearer said so), so no new inference is invented. Verified through the UI on **both** real instruments: the O2Ring file → `finger` / *O2Ring-class single-channel finger pleth*, the Verity file → `wrist` / *Polar Verity Sense (3-LED)*. **Source-scanned** because no dynamic lane can see that builder (same reasoning as `verifiedUnder`’s forbidden-writer rule), with a control that the DSP sibling still emits the pair. **Negative-controlled twice — one by accident worth keeping:** the scan first went RED against the FIXED source because the explanatory comment quoted the offending literal, proving it can see the string at all. Also records that `recording.device` has **no machine consumer** (searched integrator/overdex/unifier), so the fix moves no fusion arithmetic. | Brief *(DONE 2026-08-01)* |
| [`OXYDEX-SPO2-SERIES-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/OXYDEX-SPO2-SERIES-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **The oximeter's primary signal never left the node.** OxyDex exported SpO₂ **nowhere** — its whole `timeseries` block was 89 five-minute epochs of `{hr, motionIndex}` for a night in which the device recorded **~26,500 samples**: a ~300× reduction applied at the **export boundary, not by the sensor**, which is why the apnea→desaturation transit resolved 3 nights of 39. Ships `timeseries.spo2 = {hz:1, n, values[]}` on a uniform grid from `startEpochMs` (measured 2026-07-26: 26,546 samples, 98.7 % non-null, **298×** the epoch count). Additive — `epochs` untouched, block ABSENT rather than empty so a reader can tell "predates the field" from "no usable SpO₂"; a second the device never reported is `null`, never `0` (the most severe desaturation physically possible) and never carried forward (which reads as stable oxygen). **1 Hz kept although measurably oversampled** — 6.5 % of adjacent seconds differ, median identical-run 8 s, 2 s bins would cost ≤1 pp — because that bandwidth is one subject / one oximeter / one night, and a bin size baked into an export contract degrades any faster patient invisibly. **No committed fixture carries a timeseries block**, so nothing moved and the field would have shipped ungated on a green suite that never ran it — hence `oxyBuildSpo2Series` is exposed purely to be gated (11 assertions). | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`DESAT-ONSET-FIDUCIAL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md`](briefs/DESAT-ONSET-FIDUCIAL-2026-07-31-BRIEF.md) | **A desaturation has two instants and the export carried one.** `desat_event.tMs` is the NADIR — right for scoring, wrong for timing, because the event begins when saturation starts falling and bottoms out a desaturation-duration later, so any correlation against it measured the coupling PLUS that duration. Measured paired on the corpus: transit from the nadir is **19 s longer** than from the onset, and that 19 s is the desaturation itself. `startTMs`/`endTMs` were already computed and stamped from the parsed rows, then **discarded at the export** — same loss, same boundary as the SpO₂ series. Now on `meta` as `onsetTMs`/`endTMs`; `tMs` unchanged. Verified **496/496** desat events over 36 nights. **Fixes the fiducial, not the yield** — still 2 nights of 36, because `desat_event` is artifact-gated to the clinical ODI definition (7–15/night); density comes from `timeseries.spo2` (10 nights) instead. That is the real argument for exporting the series: **a node exporting only its own clinical events forces every consumer to inherit a definition chosen for a different purpose.** No committed fixture carries a `desat_event` at all — third change today that moves no fixture and would have shipped ungated on a green suite. | Brief *(DONE 2026-07-31)* |
| [`CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md`](briefs/CROSS-DEVICE-CLOCK-SKEW-2026-07-29-BRIEF.md) | **The CPAP's clock is ~39 minutes slow, and nothing in the suite can tell.** Every CPAP-to-other-node event comparison the Integrator has made was aligned against it; the fusion did not fail loudly, it found no overlap — indistinguishable from *there was no overlap*.
